TL;DR: This paper presents a loosely coupled factor graph optimization (FGO) framework that fuses GNSS/pseudolite least-squares solutions with IMU data, achieving 22.8% to 41.3% reduction in mean 3D error in low GNSS visibility scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In GNSS-degraded environments, pseudolites provide additional signal sources to enhance positioning performance, but their integration in optimization-based frameworks remains limited.

Method: A loosely coupled factor graph optimization (FGO) framework that fuses GNSS/PL least-squares (LS) solutions with inertial measurement unit (IMU) data, evaluated in low GNSS visibility scenarios with four high-elevation GNSS satellites and up to two PL transmitters over an 80-second window.

Result: FGO achieves a 22.8% to 41.3% reduction in mean 3D error compared to standard LS methods; incorporating PL transmitters further improves positioning accuracy compared to a GNSS-IMU baseline, with performance depending on geometry.

Conclusion: The proposed FGO framework effectively fuses GNSS/PL LS solutions with IMU data to improve positioning accuracy in GNSS-degraded environments, with PL transmitters providing additional benefits that are geometry-dependent.

Abstract: In Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS)-degraded environments, pseudolites (PLs) provide additional signal sources to enhance positioning performance, but their integration in optimization-based frameworks remains limited. This paper presents a loosely coupled factor graph optimization (FGO) framework that fuses the GNSS/PL least-squares (LS) solutions with inertial measurement unit (IMU) data. The evaluation considers low GNSS visibility scenarios with four high-elevation GNSS satellites and up to two PL transmitters over an 80~s window. FGO achieves a 22.8\% to 41.3\% reduction in mean 3D error compared to standard LS methods. Compared to a GNSS-IMU baseline, incorporating PL transmitters further improves positioning accuracy, with performance depending on geometry.

</details>


### [30] [Learning, locomotion, and navigation of soft synthetic snakes in three-dimensional, heterogeneous environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24985)
*Xiaotian Zhang,Ali Albazroun,Tixian Wang,Songyuan Cui,Prashant G. Mehta,Mattia Gazzola*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A computational framework combining bio-inspired actuation/sensing models with reinforcement learning enables soft synthetic snakes to navigate unstructured 3D terrains by learning locomotion primitives in simplified environments and composing them into adaptive strategies for complex landscapes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Limbless terrestrial animals demonstrate exceptional locomotor versatility and control that current engineered systems cannot match, motivating the need for a computational framework to enable soft synthetic snakes to navigate unstructured, heterogeneous 3D terrains.

Method: Bio-inspired actuation and sensing models reduce control complexity for high-degree-of-freedom continuum bodies, integrated into a reinforcement learning architecture. Training first occurs in simplified homogeneous terrains to learn locomotion primitives, which are then composed into adaptive strategies for complex landscapes.

Result: Robust deployment demonstrated in high-fidelity 3D environments reconstructed from real-world imaging, achieving reliable navigation.

Conclusion: This work provides a physically-realistic simulation platform and practical insights for controlling continuum systems in natural terrains.

Abstract: Limbless terrestrial animals exhibit exceptional locomotor versatility and control, currently unmatched by engineered counterparts. Here, we introduce a computational framework that enables soft synthetic snakes to navigate unstructured, heterogeneous 3D terrains. Our approach is grounded in bio-inspired actuation and sensing models that reduce the control complexity inherent to high-degree-of-freedom, continuum bodies. These models are integrated into a reinforcement learning architecture to derive environment-traversing policies. Training first occurs in simplified, homogeneous terrains to learn locomotion primitives. These are then composed into adaptive strategies for complex landscapes. We demonstrate robustness by deploying a snake in high-fidelity 3D environments reconstructed from real-world imaging, achieving reliable navigation. Overall, this work provides a physically-realistic simulation platform and practical insights for the control of continuum systems in natural terrains.

</details>


### [31] [Stiffness Optimization for Concentrated Bending in Magnetically Actuated Catheters: Maintaining Steerability under Gradient Stiffness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25005)
*Jiewen Tan,Junnan Xue,Shing Shin Cheng,Shuang Song,Erli Lyu,Jiaole Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A stiffness-optimized multi-segment magnetically actuated catheter (SO-MAC) addresses the trade-off between pushability and steerability using a gradient-stiffness architecture and decoupled steering-advancement mechanism, achieving stable proximal bending and straight distal advancement with high curvature (180° at 3 mm radius) and precise navigation in bronchial phantoms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The trade-off between efficient pushability (force transmission) and proximally concentrated bending (steerability) in magnetically actuated soft catheters: high stiffness improves force transmission but reduces steerability, while low stiffness enables bending but increases kinking/buckling risk under compressive loads.

Method: Proposes a stiffness-optimized multi-segment magnetically actuated catheter (SO-MAC) combining a decoupled steering-advancement mechanism with a gradient-stiffness architecture. The proximal segment concentrates bending stably during advancement, while the distal section passively self-straightens via elastic recovery of the spring backbone, resisting friction-induced kinking/buckling.

Result: 1.5 mm-diameter SO-MAC achieved up to 180° steering with 3 mm bending radius at 10 mm tip; average shape error 1.39±0.56 mm; steering-pivot error 0.35±0.10 mm; pivot remained stable over 0-180° combined steering and advancement. Visual feedback control confirmed robust navigation through highly curved, bifurcating bronchial paths.

Conclusion: The gradient-stiffness design effectively decouples steering and propulsion, enabling both high curvature steerability and efficient pushability in a small-diameter magnetically actuated catheter, with demonstrated clinical relevance for navigating complex bronchial anatomy.

Abstract: Achieving both efficient pushability (propulsion transmission) and proximally concentrated bending for steerability is challenging for magnetically actuated soft catheters: higher axial/bending stiffness improves force transmission but reduces steerability, whereas lower stiffness enables large, proximally concentrated bending yet increases kinking/buckling risk under compressive push loads. To address this trade-off, we propose a stiffness-optimized multi-segment magnetically actuated catheter (SO-MAC) that integrates a decoupled steering-advancement mechanism with a gradient-stiffness architecture. The SO-MAC concentrates bending about a stable proximal pivot during advancement while the distal section passively self-straightens to transmit propulsion, aided by the optimized stiffness distribution and elastic recovery of the spring backbone against friction-induced kinking/buckling. Over $0{-}180^{\circ}$ combined steering and advancement, the pivot remained stable and the distal tip advanced near-straight toward the target direction. A 1.5 mm-diameter SO-MAC achieved up to $180^{\circ}$ steering with a 3 mm bending radius at its 10 mm tip, with an average shape error of $1.39 \pm 0.56$ mm and a steering-pivot error of $0.35 \pm 0.10$ mm. Visual feedback control in a bronchial phantom further confirmed robust navigation through highly curved, bifurcating paths.

</details>


### [32] [Convex-Neural RRT*: Fast and Reliable Learning-Guided Sampling for High-Quality Robot Path Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25006)
*Hichem Cheriet,Badra Khellat Kihel,Samira Chouraqui,Bara J. Emran*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Convex-Neural RRT* is an enhanced RRT* variant that uses neural predictions to extract convex candidate regions for focused sampling, achieving 30-75% faster computation than neural-guided variants and 88-98% faster than LTA*, with ~5% path length reduction and >99% success rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sampling-based path planning algorithms like RRT* offer probabilistic completeness but require many iterations to produce high-quality solutions, motivating the need for methods that accelerate convergence while preserving global exploration guarantees.

Method: The method predicts informative waypoint regions using a neural network, extracts convex candidate regions from these predictions, and guides RRT* sampling to focus exploration on geometrically relevant areas while maintaining global coverage.

Result: 30-75% computation time reduction vs. neural-guided variants, 88-98% reduction vs. LTA*, ~5% average path length reduction vs. classical RRT* with larger gains in complex environments, and >99% success rate across varying obstacle densities.

Conclusion: Convex-guided neural sampling effectively balances computational efficiency and solution quality, making it suitable for time-sensitive robotic navigation tasks.

Abstract: Sampling-based algorithms for robot path planning offer probabilistic completeness and strong empirical convergence properties across environments with diverse obstacle configurations. However, in practice, these methods often require many iterations to obtain high-quality solutions. This paper proposes Convex-Neural RRT*, an enhanced RRT* variant that incorporates neural guidance to predict informative waypoint regions near high-quality paths. Convex candidate regions are extracted from these predictions, enabling the planner to concentrate exploration on geometrically relevant areas while preserving global exploration. The proposed algorithm is evaluated against Neural RRT*, Neural Informed RRT*, classical RRT*, and LTA* across three environment types and 18 benchmark maps. Experimental results show that Convex-Neural RRT* reduces computation time by 30-75% compared to neural-guided variants and up to 88-98% relative to LTA*, while achieving an average path length reduction of approximately 5% compared to classical RRT*, with larger improvements observed in complex environments. The method also maintains an overall success rate above 99% across varying obstacle densities. These findings indicate that convex-guided neural sampling provides an effective balance between computational efficiency and solution quality, supporting its applicability to time-sensitive robotic navigation tasks.

</details>


### [33] [Performance Comparison of Classical and Neural Sampling Algorithms for Robotic Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25010)
*Hichem Cheriet,Badra Khellat Kihel,Samira Chouraqui*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper implements and evaluates RRT*, Neural RRT*, and Neural Informed RRT* on environments with convex/concave obstacles of varying densities. Neural-guided planners produce up to 14% shorter and 55-75% smoother paths than RRT*, with Neural Informed RRT* achieving the best overall performance, demonstrating AI-guided sampling's effectiveness for autonomous navigation despite slightly increased computation time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve autonomous navigation efficiency by integrating AI into sampling-based motion planning, addressing limitations of conventional RRT* in path quality and trajectory smoothness.

Method: Three algorithms (RRT*, Neural RRT*, Neural Informed RRT*) are implemented and evaluated on environments with convex and concave obstacles at different obstacle densities.

Result: Neural-guided planners produce up to 14% shorter paths and 55-75% smoother trajectories compared to RRT*; Neural Informed RRT* achieves the best overall performance in path length and smoothness, with a slight increase in computation time.

Conclusion: AI-guided sampling strategies effectively improve reliability and trajectory efficiency in robotic and UAV navigation, highlighting AI's growing importance in real-time robotic path planning.

Abstract: Integrating artificial intelligence (AI) into sampling-based motion planning provides new possibilities for improving autonomous navigation efficiency. In this paper, three algorithms, namely RRT*, Neural RRT*, and Neural Informed RRT*, are implemented and evaluated on environments containing convex and concave obstacles with different obstacle densities. The obtained results indicate that neural-guided planners improve path quality, producing up to 14\% shorter paths and 55--75\% smoother trajectories compared with the conventional RRT* algorithm. Among the evaluated methods, Neural Informed RRT* achieves the best overall performance in terms of path length and trajectory smoothness. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of AI-guided sampling strategies for improving reliability and trajectory efficiency in robotic and UAV navigation, despite a slight increase in computation time. Overall, the study highlights the growing importance of artificial intelligence in real-time robotic path planning applications.

</details>


### [34] [Micro-Swarm Locomotion Optimization in Dynamic Flow using Multi-Objective Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25025)
*Josef Berman,Oren Gal*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A hybrid CFD-Multi-Objective MARL framework with PCGrad surgery enables 16 magnetically actuated micro-robots to learn physically consistent swarm control in pulsatile arterial flow, achieving emergent collective behaviors (hydrodynamic throttling, ratchet repositioning) and significant improvements in progress, energy efficiency, and smoothness over baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coordinating micro-robotic swarms in physiologically realistic, time-dependent fluid environments remains unsolved for biomedical and environmental applications.

Method: Hybrid CFD-Multi-Objective MARL framework coupling a high-fidelity incompressible Navier-Stokes solver with decentralized proximal policy optimization, using PCGrad surgery to resolve gradient conflicts among three objectives (upstream progression, energy conservation, motion smoothness).

Result: Converged policy achieves progress reward 6.5-7.0, energy efficiency 0.63-0.65, near-maximum smoothness 0.97-0.99; without PCGrad, energy and smoothness collapse to near-zero within 10K steps with oscillatory progress. Three emergent behavioral phases observed: two-layer hydrodynamic throttling, cycle-synchronized ratchet mechanism, individualized final approach.

Conclusion: Time-dependent fluid-agent interactions can be captured directly within multi-objective RL loops, offering a physically grounded paradigm for micro-swarm control in biomedical navigation, environmental monitoring, and industrial microfluidics.

Abstract: Coordinating micro-robotic swarms in physiologically realistic, time-dependent fluid environments remains an unsolved challenge for biomedical and environmental applications. We present a hybrid Computational Fluid Dynamics - Multi-Objective Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning framework that directly couples a high-fidelity incompressible Navier-Stokes solver with decentralized proximal policy optimization to learn physically consistent swarm control strategies in oscillatory flow. Sixteen magnetically actuated micro-robots navigate a pulsatile arterial waveform, simultaneously optimizing upstream progression, energy conservation, and motion smoothness, reconciled using PCGrad surgery. Without PCGrad, energy efficiency and smoothness rewards collapse to near zero within 10,000 training steps while progress exhibits persistent large-amplitude oscillations, confirming that gradient conflict resolution is a structural requirement rather than an optional refinement in this domain. The converged policy achieves a progress reward of 6.5-7.0, a sustained energy efficiency of 0.63-0.65, and near-maximum smoothness (0.97-0.99), representing improvements over brute-force baselines on the primary objective while both baselines yield negative energy efficiency throughout. Training reveals three emergent behavioral phases: a collective two-layer hydrodynamic throttling formation that suppresses peak channel velocities during forward flow, a cycle-synchronized ratchet mechanism that exploits flow reversals for upstream repositioning, and an individualized final approach as agents near the success boundary. These results establish that time-dependent fluid-agent interactions can be captured directly within multi-objective reinforcement learning loops, offering a physically grounded paradigm for micro-swarm control in biomedical navigation, environmental monitoring, and industrial microfluidics.

</details>


### [35] [ParkingWorld: End-to-End Autonomous Parking Reinforcement Learning from Corrective Experience in 3DGS Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25029)
*Zhengcheng Yu,Changze Li,Haoran Liu,Tong Qin*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents CIL-SERL, a correction-in-the-loop sample-efficient reinforcement learning framework for end-to-end autonomous parking, trained in a photorealistic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) parking simulator. It uses a novel multi-level replay buffer that stores standard RL rollouts, human corrections, failed trajectories, and rollback segments to improve learning. Experiments in simulation and on physical vehicles show improved parking success rate, efficiency, and safety.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autonomous parking requires precise low-speed maneuvering in narrow, cluttered environments. Imitation learning needs massive expert demonstrations and generalizes poorly, while traditional RL suffers from excessive training overhead, inefficient exploration, and failures in challenging settings. A more sample-efficient and generalizable solution is needed.

Method: The CIL-SERL framework trains an end-to-end parking policy in a photorealistic 3DGS simulator. It features a multi-level replay buffer that hierarchically organizes standard RL rollouts, human corrective interventions, failed exploration trajectories, and rollback-based correction segments to enable structured sampling and targeted learning.

Result: Substantial improvements in parking success rate, operational efficiency, and safety performance across diverse scenarios in both simulation and physical vehicle testing.

Conclusion: The proposed CIL-SERL framework is effective and practically applicable for end-to-end autonomous parking, addressing key limitations of imitation learning and traditional RL methods.

Abstract: Autonomous parking demands precise low-speed maneuvering within narrow, cluttered, and highly constrained environments, where vehicles must navigate tight spaces while avoiding static obstacles and complex geometric boundaries. Unlike imitation learning, which typically requires massive volumes of high-quality expert demonstrations to converge to a stable policy and often suffers from limited generalization to unseen scenarios, traditional reinforcement learning (RL) methods face persistent challenges including excessive training overhead, inefficient exploration, and even failure to learn viable parking strategies in challenging settings. To address these limitations, this paper presents a correction-in-the-loop sample-efficient reinforcement learning (CIL-SERL) framework for end-to-end autonomous parking, which is entirely trained in a photorealistic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) parking simulator that enables high-fidelity digital reconstruction of real-world scenes. Inspired by error-correction notebooks used in learning practice, we design a novel multi-level replay buffer mechanism. These buffers hierarchically organize and store standard RL rollouts, human corrective interventions, failed exploration trajectories, and rollback-based correction segments in separate yet interconnected memory regions, facilitating structured sampling and targeted learning during training. The proposed framework is systematically evaluated in both the 3DGS simulation environment and a physical vehicle platform. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that our method achieves substantial improvements in parking success rate, operational efficiency, and safety performance across diverse scenarios, validating the effectiveness and practical applicability of the proposed CIL-SERL-based end-to-end autonomous parking solution.

</details>


### [36] [RAMBA: 4D Radar Mapping by Bundle Adjustment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25041)
*Jianzhu Huai,Yiwen Chen,Binliang Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: RAMBA is a radar bundle-adjustment framework that jointly refines 4D radar frames using geometric residuals, IMU preintegration, and ego-velocity constraints to achieve globally consistent mapping, outperforming radar-inertial odometry and pose-graph optimization baselines on real-world datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Offline global map refinement for 4D radar remains underexplored despite promising online state estimation from radar-inertial odometry, motivating the need for a bundle-adjustment framework to achieve globally consistent 4D radar mapping.

Method: RAMBA jointly refines radar frame states via covariance-weighted geometric residuals (extending pairwise GICP to multi-frame optimization with voxel-based correspondences), IMU preintegration factors, and radar ego-velocity constraints, while enforcing temporal consistency and supporting loop-closure constraints.

Result: On ColoRadar and SNAIL Radar datasets, RAMBA improves map consistency and usually enhances trajectory accuracy over radar-inertial odometry and pose-graph optimization baselines.

Conclusion: RAMBA demonstrates that offline bundle adjustment with geometric, inertial, and Doppler-derived constraints yields superior global consistency for 4D radar mapping compared to existing online and pose-graph approaches.

Abstract: 4D radar is increasingly attractive for robotic mapping because it provides range, azimuth, elevation, and Doppler measurements while remaining robust in adverse visibility conditions. Although recent radar and radar--inertial odometry methods have achieved promising online state estimation performance, offline global map refinement for 4D radar remains underexplored. This paper presents RAMBA, a radar bundle-adjustment framework for globally consistent 4D radar mapping. Given initial poses and radar frames from a radar--inertial odometry front-end, RAMBA jointly refines radar frame states using covariance-weighted geometric residuals, IMU preintegration factors, and radar ego-velocity constraints. The geometric residuals extend pairwise GICP to a multi-frame optimization by forming voxel-based correspondences across selected frames and weighting each residual with point covariances. To improve robustness against drift and revisits, RAMBA enforces temporal consistency during correspondence formation while explicitly supporting loop-closure constraints. Experiments on the ColoRadar and SNAIL Radar datasets show that RAMBA improves map consistency and usually enhances trajectory accuracy over radar--inertial odometry and pose-graph optimization baselines.

</details>


### [37] [X-DiffVLA: X-Embodied Diffusion Action Heads for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25044)
*Boyu Li,Chaoyi Xu,Haoqi Yuan,Xinrun Xu,Börje F. Karlsson,Dongbin Zhao,Haoran Li,Zongqing Lu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: X-DiffVLA is a diffusion-based VLA model with a unified cross-embodied action head that leverages Embodiment Forcing and Morphological Tree Diffusion to enable policy learning across heterogeneous embodiments, achieving SOTA performance with 15.3% and 12.5% improvements on RoboCasa and Isaac Gym.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing VLA models rely on embodiment-specific fine-tuning to achieve strong downstream performance, which severely limits their generalization capability and restricts knowledge transfer across different embodiments performing similar tasks.

Method: Proposes X-DiffVLA with three key components: (1) a unified cross-embodied action head for heterogeneous action spaces, (2) Embodiment Forcing—a classifier-free guidance technique that implicitly steers action generation toward embodiment-specific functional components without explicit supervision, and (3) Morphological Tree Diffusion that strengthens behavioral correlations across diverse end-effectors to maximize transferability of heterogeneous demonstrations.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance with 15.3% improvement on RoboCasa and 12.5% improvement on Isaac Gym across embodiments ranging from grippers to dexterous hands. Real-world evaluations validate robustness and scalability.

Conclusion: X-DiffVLA effectively addresses cross-embodied policy learning by leveraging diffusion models to capture diversity and latent correlations in cross-embodied datasets, enabling scalable policy learning without embodiment-specific fine-tuning.

Abstract: Learning universal policies from cross-embodied data remains a fundamental challenge in robotics. Although Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are pre-trained on large and diverse datasets, they typically rely on embodiment-specific fine-tuning to achieve strong performance in downstream tasks. This requirement severely limits their generalization capability and restricts knowledge transfer across embodiments performing similar tasks. To overcome these limitations, we focus on cross-embodied settings with shared robotic bases and heterogeneous end-effectors, and propose X-DiffVLA, a diffusion-based VLA model featuring a unified cross-embodied action head. X-DiffVLA can leverage the generative strengths of diffusion models to capture both the diversity and latent correlations in cross-embodied datasets. Specifically, we introduce Embodiment Forcing, a classifier-free guidance technique to implicitly steer action generation toward embodiment-specific functional components, capturing fine-grained structural nuances without explicit supervision. In addition, a Morphological Tree Diffusion approach is designed to strengthen behavioral correlations across diverse end-effectors, maximizing the transferability of heterogeneous demonstrations. Experimental results across RoboCasa and Isaac Gym, covering different embodiments from grippers to dexterous hands, show that X-DiffVLA achieves state-of-the-art performance, with improvements of 15.3% and 12.5%, respectively. Real-world evaluations further validate the robustness of the proposed framework and its effectiveness in scalable cross-embodied policy learning.

</details>


### [38] [A Decentralized LiDAR-SLAM System with Certifiably Optimal Pose Graph Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25051)
*Baoshan Song,Feng Huang,Li-Ta Hsu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper presents the first decentralized LiDAR-SLAM system with a certifiably optimal PGO backend using RBCD, achieving globally consistent trajectory estimation without accurate initial guesses and improving RMSE by up to 48.9% over DiSCo-SLAM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Decentralized multi-robot LiDAR-SLAM faces challenges in maintaining global consistency due to suboptimal convergence and long-term inconsistency in existing local-search or one-time alignment frameworks, especially in large-scale or degenerate environments.

Method: Integrates a certifiably optimal Pose Graph Optimization (PGO) backend using the Riemannian Block Coordinate Descent (RBCD) algorithm into a decentralized LiDAR-SLAM system, eliminating the need for accurate initial guesses.

Result: The proposed framework achieves superior robustness, improving trajectory RMSE by up to 48.9% compared to the state-of-the-art DiSCo-SLAM.

Conclusion: The decentralized LiDAR-SLAM with certifiably optimal PGO backend ensures globally consistent trajectory estimation and outperforms existing methods in robustness.

Abstract: Decentralized multi-robot LiDAR-SLAM is essential for collaborative missions but faces significant challenges in maintaining global consistency. Existing frameworks predominantly rely on local-search optimization or one-time coordinate alignment, which are prone to suboptimal convergence and long-term inconsistency, especially in large-scale or degenerate environments. To address these limitations, this paper presents the first decentralized LiDAR-SLAM system that integrates a state-of-the-art certifiably optimal Pose Graph Optimization (PGO) backend. By leveraging the Riemannian Block Coordinate Descent (RBCD) algorithm, our system ensures globally consistent trajectory estimation without requiring accurate initial guesses. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves superior robustness, improving trajectory RMSE by up to 48.9% compared to the state-of-the-art DiSCo-SLAM.

</details>


### [39] [Soft Pneumatic Actuators for Soft Robotics: A Motion-Based Review of Actuation Mechanisms and Performance Trade-offs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25109)
*Mohammed Abboodi*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This review categorizes soft pneumatic actuators by design strategies for four motion classes (linear, bending, twisting, omnidirectional), analyzing how structural features affect performance metrics, and identifies key considerations for actuator selection and comparison across studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Soft pneumatic actuators' performance depends on multiple design factors beyond pressure alone, making it difficult to compare results across studies or select suitable mechanisms for specific applications as the literature expands.

Method: The review examines actuators by four motion classes (linear, bending, twisting, omnidirectional), analyzing structural features (braid angle, fold geometry, fiber orientation, chamber arrangement, asymmetry, constraint layers) and their impact on performance metrics. It also identifies key comparison conditions (pressure, loading, size, supply, hysteresis).

Result: Actuators with similar motion outputs can differ substantially in design requirements, pneumatic demand, and practical suitability. Design priorities differ for compact, efficient, repeatable, and deployable systems in wearable, biomedical, and mobile robotics.

Conclusion: The review provides a structured framework for selecting and comparing soft pneumatic actuators based on motion class and design features, highlighting trade-offs that guide application-specific design choices.

Abstract: Soft pneumatic actuators are widely used in soft robotics because they can produce large motions while remaining compliant enough to interact safely with objects, environments, and the human body. However, their performance is not solely determined by pressure. Instead, the response depends on the way the actuator is built, including the shape of its chambers, the placement of reinforcements, the use of folds, material stiffness, and the constraints that guide its deformation. As the literature has expanded, it has become more difficult to determine which mechanism is most suitable for a given application and which reported results can be compared across studies. This review examines soft pneumatic actuators according to the design strategies used to generate four motion classes: linear, bending, twisting, and omnidirectional actuation. For each class, it analyzes the structural features that define the deformation path, including braid angle, fold geometry, fiber orientation, chamber arrangement, structural asymmetry, and internal constraint layers. It then discusses how the design choice affect motion output, force generation, air demand, repeatability, durability, fabrication difficulty, and robotic integration. The review further identifies key conditions that must be considered when selecting or comparing actuators, including pressure, loading condition, actuator size, pneumatic supply, and hysteresis This approach helps explain why actuators with similar motion outputs may differ substantially in design requirements, pneumatic demand, and practical suitability. It also highlights the design priorities needed for compact, efficient, repeatable, and deployable soft pneumatic systems in wearable, biomedical, and mobile robotic applications.

</details>


### [40] [InvariantCloud: A Globally Invariant, Uniquely Indexed Point Cloud Framework for Robust 6-DoF Tactile Pose Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25216)
*Pengfei Ye,Yuxiang Ma,Yi Zhou,Wei Chen,Wenzhen Dong,Molong Duan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: InvariantCloud is a 6-DoF tactile pose estimation framework using globally invariant point cloud registration of surface marker constellations, achieving superior yaw tracking and re-localization for long-sequence robotic manipulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for high-fidelity tactile perception and accurate 6-DoF object pose estimation to enable precise robotic manipulation, addressing limitations in estimating yaw (Z-axis) rotation and cumulative drift in existing approaches.

Method: A one-shot globally invariant point cloud registration framework based on surface marker constellations on vision-based tactile sensors, leveraging global invariance to suppress cumulative drift.

Result: Superior yaw tracking accuracy and re-localization repeatability compared to existing benchmarks, demonstrating precision and robustness in long-sequence manipulation tasks.

Conclusion: InvariantCloud overcomes long-standing limitations in yaw rotation estimation and cumulative drift, establishing a robust foundation for tactile-based robotic manipulation.

Abstract: Recent advances in imitation learning and vision-language models highlight the need for high-fidelity tactile perception, with 6-DoF tactile object pose estimation providing a crucial foundation for precise robotic manipulation. We introduce InvariantCloud, a 6-DoF pose estimation framework that leverages the global invariance of surface marker constellations on vision-based tactile sensors. In contrast to recent approaches, our one-shot globally invariant point cloud registration suppresses cumulative drift and overcomes long-standing limitations in accurately estimating yaw (Z-axis) rotation. Experimental verifications show that InvariantCloud achieves superior yaw tracking accuracy and re-localization repeatability compared to existing benchmarks, demonstrating its precision and robustness in long-sequence manipulation tasks.

</details>


### [41] [FusionCore: A 23-State Unscented Kalman Filter for IMU, Wheel Encoder, GPS, and Visual SLAM Fusion in ROS 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25239)
*Manan Kharwar*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: FusionCore is an open-source ROS 2 sensor fusion package that fuses IMU, wheel odometry, GPS, and VSLAM using a 23-state UKF, achieving lower ATE than robot_localization on 10/12 NCLT sequences while remaining numerically stable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing open-source sensor fusion solutions like robot_localization suffer from numerical divergence and lack features such as online wheel bias estimation, automatic noise adaptation, and native ECEF GPS handling, motivating the development of a more robust and feature-complete alternative.

Method: A 23-state Unscented Kalman Filter (UKF) fusing IMU, wheel odometry, GPS, and VSLAM at 100 Hz. Key innovations include: (1) 23rd state for online wheel encoder yaw rate bias estimation using GPS heading cross-covariance; (2) native ECEF GPS handling; (3) per-sensor Mahalanobis chi-squared outlier gating; (4) automatic sensor noise covariance adaptation from innovation sequence; (5) VSLAM pose fusion with map reinitialization recovery.

Result: Evaluated on 12 NCLT sequences (55-92 min each). FusionCore achieves lower ATE on 10 of 12 sequences (1.2x to 22.2x improvement). The robot_localization UKF diverges numerically on all 12 sequences.

Conclusion: FusionCore outperforms robot_localization in both accuracy and numerical stability for multi-sensor fusion, and is released as open-source under Apache 2.0.

Abstract: We present FusionCore, an open-source ROS 2 sensor fusion package that fuses IMU, wheel encoder odometry, GPS, and Visual SLAM pose into a single 100 Hz odometry stream using a 23-state Unscented Kalman Filter (UKF). The 23rd state is an online estimate of the wheel encoder's systematic yaw rate bias, identified through GPS heading cross-covariance and subtracted during GPS blackouts to reduce heading drift in coast mode. FusionCore also estimates gyroscope and accelerometer biases as explicit filter states, handles GPS natively in ECEF without a separate coordinate projection node, applies per-sensor Mahalanobis chi-squared outlier gating calibrated to measurement degrees of freedom, and adapts sensor noise covariance automatically from the innovation sequence. VSLAM pose fusion enables GPS-denied operation with any visual odometry or SLAM system, including automatic recovery from map reinitialization. We evaluate against robot_localization on twelve full-length sequences (55-92 min each) from the NCLT public dataset. FusionCore achieves lower Absolute Trajectory Error (ATE) on ten of twelve sequences, with improvements ranging from 1.2x to 22.2x on winning sequences. The robot_localization UKF diverges numerically on all twelve sequences. FusionCore is available at https://github.com/manankharwar/fusioncore under the Apache 2.0 license.

</details>


### [42] [GreenSeg: Ground Segmentation Algorithm for Agricultural Robots in Mediterranean Greenhouses using RGB-D Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25279)
*Fernando Cañadas-Aránega,José C. Moreno,José L. Blanco-Claraco*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: GreenSeg is a RGB-D perception framework for autonomous navigation in Mediterranean greenhouses, using dual-layer validation (global plane fitting + curvature filter and seed-point Region Growing) to handle narrow aisles, heterogeneous terrains, and optical interference from polyethylene covers. It outperforms benchmark methods by up to 11.58% in Recall and 19.24% in mIoU during rotational maneuvers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mediterranean greenhouse agriculture faces automation challenges due to narrow aisles, heterogeneous terrains, and severe optical interference from polyethylene covers causing specular reflections and ghost points. Existing autonomous navigation solutions rely on expensive 3D LiDAR systems that are economically unscalable for most facilities.

Method: GreenSeg introduces a dual-layer validation strategy for RGB-D perception: (1) robust global plane fitting combined with a surface curvature filter for terrain adaptability, and (2) seed-point-based Region Growing constraint to ensure spatial continuity of the navigable plane. Validated on the AGRICOBIOT I platform across four diurnal scenarios with varying solar elevations.

Result: GreenSeg consistently outperforms benchmark segmentation methods, achieving peak improvements of 11.58% in mean Recall and 19.24% in mIoU during critical rotational maneuvers at end of corridors.

Conclusion: The proposed algorithm enables stable and safe autonomous navigation in unstructured, dynamic agricultural environments that are subject to budget constraints and sensitive to lighting conditions, offering a cost-effective alternative to 3D LiDAR systems.

Abstract: Greenhouse agriculture in the Mediterranean region faces significant automation challenges due to its unique structural and environmental constraints. These environments are characterized by extremely narrow aisles, heterogeneous terrains ranging from concrete to tilled soil and severe optical interference caused by polyethylene covers, which induce specular reflections and "ghost points" in depth sensors. While autonomous navigation is essential for digitizing agricultural tasks, traditional solutions often rely on expensive 3D LiDAR systems that are economically unscalable for most facilities. To address this, this paper presents GreenSeg, a robust perception framework for autonomous navigation using RGB-D sensing. The proposed method introduces a dual-layer validation strategy: a robust global plane fitting combined with a surface curvature filter for terrain adaptability, and a seed-point-based Region Growing constraint to ensure the spatial continuity of the navigable plane. Experimental validation was conducted using the AGRICOBIOT I platform across four diurnal scenarios with varying solar elevations. The results show that GreenSeg consistently outperforms benchmark segmentation methods, achieving peak improvements of 11.58% in mean Recall and 19.24% in mIoU during critical rotational maneuvers at the end of corridors. These findings confirm that the proposed algorithm enables stable and safe autonomous navigation in unstructured, dynamic agricultural environments that are subject to budget constraints and sensitive to lighting conditions.

</details>


### [43] [Parallel Differentiable Reachability for Learning and Planning with Certified Neural Dynamics and Controllers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25346)
*Keyi Shen,Glen Chou*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A parallelizable, differentiable reachability framework in JAX for neural network dynamics and control, combining Taylor-model flowpipe construction with CROWN-style linear bound propagation, enabling certified training and reachability-aware sampling-based MPC with gradient-based refinement. Demonstrated on manipulation and quadrotor tasks up to 72D with online planning and certified over-approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing reachability tools for closed-loop NN systems are often non-differentiable, overly conservative, or too slow for modern learning and online planning pipelines. A differentiable, parallelizable framework is needed to bridge formal verification with learning and control.

Method: Combines Taylor-model flowpipe construction with CROWN-style linear bound propagation through a unified representation preserving affine dependencies, implemented in JAX with GPU-batched computation and automatic differentiation. Two applications built on this: (i) certified training for reachability-friendly models, and (ii) reachability-aware sampling-based MPC with gradient-based refinement.

Result: Demonstrated on non-prehensile manipulation and quadrotor tasks including hardware and higher-dimensional evaluations (up to 72D), achieving practical online planning while maintaining certified reachable-set over-approximations under bounded uncertainty.

Conclusion: The proposed differentiable reachability framework successfully enables both certified training and online MPC planning with formal guarantees, showing practical applicability to real-world robotic systems.

Abstract: Neural network (NN) dynamics models and control policies achieve strong performance in robotics, but providing sound guarantees under uncertainty remains difficult, especially for closed-loop NN systems. Existing reachability tools provide formal over-approximations, yet are often non-differentiable, overly conservative, or too slow for modern learning and online planning pipelines. To address this, we present a parallelizable, differentiable reachability framework in JAX for continuous- and discrete-time systems with analytical and NN-based dynamics and controllers. Our framework combines Taylor-model flowpipe construction with CROWN-style linear bound propagation through a unified representation that preserves affine dependencies while supporting GPU-batched computation and automatic differentiation. Building on this reachability primitive, we develop (i) a certified training method that encourages reachability-friendly dynamics models and controllers, and (ii) a reachability-aware sampling-based MPC scheme with gradient-based refinement. Experiments on non-prehensile manipulation and quadrotor tasks, including hardware and higher-dimensional evaluations (up to 72D), demonstrate practical online planning while maintaining certified reachable-set over-approximations under bounded uncertainty.

</details>


### [44] [Prior Policy Guided Dual-Agent Coordinated Manipulation Planning of Spacecraft-Manipulator System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25362)
*Yuhui Hu,Dong Zhou,Kaihong Ouyang,Zhongliang Yu,Jianfeng Lv,Xiangyu Shao*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A Dual-Agent Coordinated Manipulation Planning framework using prior policy-guided deep reinforcement learning with Timestep-level Expert Switching Guidance is proposed for simultaneously achieving high-precision end-effector control of a 6-DoF space manipulator and base spacecraft attitude stabilization, demonstrating superior success rates, control precision, and robustness against disturbances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The strong dynamic coupling between a space manipulator and its base spacecraft poses significant attitude stability challenges that can compromise mission safety, motivating the need for coordinated control solutions.

Method: Proposes a Dual-Agent Coordinated Manipulation Planning (DACMP) framework with a prior policy-guided Deep Reinforcement Learning algorithm incorporating the Timestep-level Expert Switching Guidance (TESG) mechanism to improve learning efficiency and global convergence.

Result: DACMP significantly outperforms baseline DRL algorithms in task success rate and control precision, with robustness validated under system constraints, environmental disturbances, and perception uncertainties.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively addresses the dynamic coupling challenge for safe and precise space manipulation, with code and simulation configurations publicly available.

Abstract: The strong dynamic coupling between the manipulator and the base poses a significant challenge to maintaining spacecraft attitude stability, potentially compromising mission safety. In this paper, we propose a Dual-Agent Coordinated Manipulation Planning (DACMP) framework that simultaneously achieves high-precision end-effector pose reaching for a 6-DoF space manipulator and attitude stabilization of the base spacecraft. To enhance learning efficiency, we present a prior policy-guided Deep Reinforcement Learning algorithm incorporating the Timestep-level Expert Switching Guidance (TESG) mechanism, thereby promoting global convergence and improving task success rates. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DACMP significantly outperforms baseline DRL algorithms in terms of task success rate and control precision. Furthermore, the robustness of DACMP is validated under various challenging scenarios, including system constraints, environmental disturbances, and perception uncertainties. The code and simulation configurations are available on GitHub: https://github.com/HIT-YuhuiHu/DACMP.

</details>


### [45] [FOUND-IT: Foundation-model-first Task-driven 3D Scene Graphs with Granularity on Demand](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25371)
*Dominic Maggio,Nicolas Gorlo,Luca Carlone*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: FOUND-IT is the first real-time approach for building hierarchical, task-driven 3D scene graphs from uncalibrated monocular camera inputs, using geometric foundation models with an added traversability head, and dynamically adapting scene granularity to evolving tasks (e.g., manipulation vs. navigation). It achieves 79% higher accuracy on the ASHiTA SG3D benchmark and runs in real-time on a Jetson Thor-equipped ground robot.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods build 3D scene graphs with fixed, predefined task sets, which fails to handle realistic scenarios where robot tasks evolve dynamically during operation. The authors aim to enable task-driven adaptability — adjusting object/region granularity on the fly — for complex loco-manipulation tasks without prior knowledge of the task list.

Method: FOUND-IT leverages geometric foundation models (e.g., VGGT) to estimate geometric attributes, and adds an extra head to reconstruct traversability information (the "places" layer). It dynamically adjusts scene graph granularity according to the current task (e.g., fine-grained knobs for manipulation vs. coarse stove for navigation). It also incorporates an agentic query mechanism for information retrieval from the scene graph.

Result: Achieves 79% higher accuracy on the ASHiTA SG3D task grounding benchmark. Demonstrates real-time performance on a ground robot using a Jetson Thor. Additionally, shows robustness by constructing 3D scene graphs from casually captured YouTube realtor apartment tours.

Conclusion: FOUND-IT provides the first real-time, task-driven 3D scene graph construction from an uncalibrated monocular camera that adapts dynamically to evolving tasks, setting a new state-of-the-art on the ASHiTA SG3D benchmark and enabling practical deployment on resource-constrained robots.

Abstract: We present the first approach to build hierarchical task-driven 3D scene graphs of arbitrary indoor or outdoor environments using an uncalibrated monocular camera in real-time. We leverage geometric foundation models to estimate geometric attributes of the scene graph (e.g., object bounding boxes), but we also observe that traversability information (the "places" layer of a scene graph) can be directly reconstructed by adding an extra head to existing geometric foundation models, like VGGT. Our approach is task-driven in the sense that we adjust the granularity of the objects and regions in the map depending on the task; for instance, during a manipulation task, our approach is able to resolve small knobs on a stove, while during a navigation task it can focus on large objects (e.g., the entire stove). However, in a major departure from related work, we consider the realistic case where the list of tasks is not predefined and fixed, but evolves as the robot operates. This naturally allows dealing with complex loco-manipulation tasks, where the robot can dynamically adjust its representation as the task unfolds. We dub the resulting approach FOUND-IT. FOUND-IT also includes an agentic approach to query information in the scene graph. In addition to achieving 79% higher accuracy on the ASHiTA SG3D task grounding benchmark, we demonstrate FOUND-IT runs in real-time on a ground robot using a Jetson Thor. Furthermore, to highlight the robustness of our method, we demonstrate constructing 3D scene graphs on casually captured realtor apartment tours from YouTube. Code will be made available upon publication.

</details>


### [46] [Decision-Making with Lightweight Confidence-Aware Language Model for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25393)
*Ruoyu Yao,Ruiguo Zhong,Pei Liu,Mingxing Peng,Rui Yang,Jun Ma*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Proposes a lightweight confidence-aware language model for autonomous driving decision-making, using multi-agent collaborative distillation and RAG to achieve SOTA success rates with low latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLMs/MLLMs show promise in autonomous driving but suffer from excessive computational overhead and high inference latency, hindering deployment in resource-constrained AD systems.

Method: (1) Multi-agent collaborative workflow (action voting, confidence assessment, summarization agents) generates confidence-annotated demonstrations via CoT reasoning. (2) Distillation into a lightweight dual-head language model predicting both decision probabilities and textual rationales. (3) Confidence-aware fine-tuning with Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) for adaptability and data efficiency.

Result: Achieves SOTA success rates in regular and long-tail scenarios on nuPlan benchmark while maintaining low inference latency.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively bridges complex multimodal intention reasoning and efficient inference, enabling practical deployment of language-model-based decision-making in autonomous driving.

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) and Multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) have demonstrated immense potential in autonomous driving (AD) by offering human-like reasoning and open-world generalization. However, the excessive computational overhead and high inference latency of these massive models severely hinder their deployment in resource-constrained AD systems. To address this challenge, we propose a novel decision-making framework utilizing a lightweight confidence-aware language model, which bridges the gap between complex multimodal intention reasoning and efficient inference. Specifically, we design a multi-agent collaborative workflow, comprising action voting, confidence assessment, and summarization agents, to generate high-quality, confidence-annotated decision demonstrations via explicit Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. These demonstrations are then distilled into a lightweight language model featuring a dual-head architecture, enabling the joint prediction of decision probabilities and the generation of textual rationales. The distillation is realized via a confidence-aware fine-tuning strategy coupled with Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) to enhance the model's adaptability and data efficiency. Comprehensive closed-loop experiments on the nuPlan benchmark demonstrate that our approach achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) success rates in both regular and long-tail scenarios while maintaining low inference latency.

</details>


### [47] [Path Following Control System of Line-of-Sight Guidance for Robotic Dolphin with Multi-Link Mechanism in Underwater Simulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25401)
*Takumi Asada,Takao Oki,Hideo Furuhashi,Kenta Tabata,Renato Miyagusuku,Koichi Ozaki*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: The paper proposes and evaluates a path following system for Biomimetic Autonomous Underwater Vehicles (BAUVs) with multi-link mechanisms using underwater simulation, enabling parameter determination and control method evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Path following systems for BAUVs with multi-link mechanisms are limited and their evaluation remains unclarified, necessitating prior simulation due to model variations across biomimetic types.

Method: The authors propose a path following system tailored for BAUVs with multi-link mechanisms and evaluate it in an underwater simulation environment.

Result: The proposed system successfully enabled path following system design for BAUV, parameter determination using a simulator, and control method evaluation.

Conclusion: The approach allows effective design, parameter tuning, and evaluation of path following control for BAUVs with multi-link mechanisms through simulation.

Abstract: Biomimetic autonomous underwater vehicle (BAUV) with multi-link mechanism is widely used in aquatic life observation and environmental surveys due to its low power consumption and high maneuverability. An environmental survey requires a path following system that automatically follows specific points. However, the path following system of BAUV is limited, and its evaluation with multi-link mechanism robots has not yet been clarified. The path following system in BAUV requires prior simulation because the model differs depending on the type of biomimetics. In this study, we propose a path following system for BAUVs with a multi-link mechanism and evaluation in underwater simulation. In this result, it was possible to design a path following system suitable for BAUV, determine parameters using a simulator, and evaluate control methods.

</details>


### [48] [How to Mitigate the Distribution Shift Problem in Robotics Control: A Robust and Adaptive Approach Based on Offline to Online Imitation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25414)
*Hyung-Suk Yoon,Seung-Woo Seo*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Proposes a robust offline-to-adaptive-online imitation learning framework with a multi-phase scheme to handle distribution shift by broadening state-action coverage via supplementary demonstrations (offline) and self-supervised adaptation from online experiences (online inference).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Distribution shift in imitation learning causes agents to fail on unseen states due to narrow state-action coverage from expert demonstrations.

Method: (1) Offline phase: uses supplementary demonstrations and a discriminator to train the policy for broader coverage. (2) Online inference phase: detects distribution shift and conducts self-supervised imitation learning from online experiences for adaptation.

Result: Outperforms baseline algorithms in MuJoCo environments, showing better robustness to distribution shift and better online adaptation.

Conclusion: The proposed framework effectively mitigates distribution shift by combining offline broadened coverage with online self-supervised adaptation.

Abstract: Distribution shift in imitation learning refers to the problem that the agent cannot plan proper actions for a state that has not been visited during the training. This problem can be largely attributed to the inherently narrow state-action coverage provided by expert demonstrations over the full environment. In this paper, we propose a robust offline to adaptive online imitation learning framework that handles the distribution shift problem in a lifelong, multi-phase scheme. In the offline learning phase, we leverage supplementary demonstrations to broaden the state-action coverage of the policy by utilizing a discriminator to effectively train the policy with supplementary demonstrations, thereby enhancing the robustness of the policy to distribution shift. In the subsequent online inference phase, our framework detects the occurrence of distribution shift and conducts self-supervised imitation learning from online experiences to adapt the policy to the online environments. Through extensive evaluations in MuJoCo environments, we demonstrate that our method exhibits better robustness to distribution shift and better adaptation performance to online environments than the baseline algorithms, which indicates superior performance of our framework against the distribution shift.

</details>


### [49] [OPAL: Omnidirectional Path-efficient Aerial 3D expLoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25423)
*Yoga Satwik Chappidi,Avideh Zakhor*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: OPAL is an exploration framework for drones that uses deliberate 360-degree yaw rotation at ambiguous branch points instead of heavy global planning, achieving shorter travel distances and higher coverage efficiency with lower computational complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autonomous exploration for mapping unknown environments requires both computational efficiency and short traversed distance. Existing methods like EDEN and FALCON are computationally complex, motivating a simpler yet effective approach.

Method: OPAL performs deliberate 360-degree yaw rotation at ambiguous branch points to gather information, then selects frontiers using model-free, LLM-based, or VLM-based variants. The vicinity search radius parameter controls the tradeoff between travel distance and exploration time.

Result: OPAL achieves shorter travel distances and higher coverage-versus-distance AUC compared to EDEN and FALCON, despite longer total exploration time due to in-place yaw rotation. On a real Modal AI drone, a variant of OPAL reduces traveled distance by up to 25% compared to FALCON.

Conclusion: OPAL demonstrates that simple, deliberate yaw-based exploration can outperform computationally complex global planning methods in terms of travel efficiency and coverage, with adjustable search radius enabling practical tradeoffs.

Abstract: Autonomous exploration is critical for robot mapping unknown environments. Desirable characteristics of exploration algorithms include compute efficiency and small traversed distance during the exploration process. Motivated by these, we present Omnidirectional Path-efficient Aerial 3D expLoration (OPAL), an exploration framework centered on deliberate 360-degree yaw rotation at ambiguous branch points rather than compute-heavy global tour planning. We devise multiple variants of OPAL to determine the frontier-selection strategy once the yaw pan is completed. One variant is model-free, while others use large language models (LLMs) or vision-language models (VLMs). We characterize the performance of these variants while varying the vicinity search radius to include frontiers in the selection process. Through simulations we find that although the time-consuming in-place yaw rotation increases total exploration time relative to more computationally complex baselines such as EDEN and FALCON, OPAL is computationally simpler and achieves shorter travel distances and higher coverage-versus-distance area under the curve. We also show that adjusting the frontier-selection search radius enables a tradeoff between travel distance and total exploration time. We verify our results on a Modal AI drone in two indoor environments by comparing OPAL against FALCON, and find that the traveled distance for a variant of OPAL to be as much as 25% lower than FALCON.

</details>


### [50] [EXPO-FT: Sample-Efficient Reinforcement Learning Finetuning for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25477)
*Perry Dong,Kuo-Han Hung,Tian Gao,Dorsa Sadigh,Chelsea Finn*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: EXPO-FT is a system for sample-efficient RL fine-tuning of pretrained VLA policies that achieves perfect task performance (30/30 successes) across challenging manipulation tasks within an average of 19.1 minutes of online robot data, outperforming prior approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: VLA models show strong generalization but lack reliability for real-world deployment; existing RL fine-tuning approaches either train from scratch without leveraging pretrained priors or fine-tune VLAs without achieving sufficient sample efficiency and success rates for practical deployment.

Method: EXPO-FT is a system for stable, sample-efficient RL fine-tuning of pretrained VLA policies, enabling efficient online adaptation from limited robot interaction data.

Result: Perfect task performance (30/30 successes) across all evaluated tasks within an average of 19.1 minutes of online robot data, outperforming prior RL-from-scratch and VLA fine-tuning approaches. Tasks include routing string lights and plug insertion, pool ball striking, and flower insertion into a wine bottle.

Conclusion: Closed the gap between pretrained VLA generalization and deployment reliability; open-source codebase released to facilitate broader adoption of RL fine-tuning for VLA models in robotics.

Abstract: The ability to efficiently and reliably learn new tasks has been a foundational challenge in robotics. Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have demonstrated strong generalization across diverse manipulation tasks, yet pretrained policies consistently fall short of the reliability required for real-world deployment. Reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning offers a promising path to bridge this gap, but existing approaches either train from scratch without fully leveraging pretrained priors, or fine-tune VLAs without achieving the sample efficiency and success rates that practical deployment demands. We present EXPO-FT, a system for stable, sample-efficient RL finetuning of pretrained VLA policies that closes this gap. Our system solves a suite of challenging manipulation tasks, including routing string lights and inserting the plug to light it up, striking a pool ball into a pocket, and inserting a flower into a wine bottle, each requiring combinations of high precision, dynamic actions, and robustness to varied initial states. Our system achieves perfect task performance (30/30 successes) across all evaluated tasks within an average of 19.1 minutes of online robot data, outperforming both prior RL-from-scratch and VLA finetuning approaches. We release an open-source codebase with the aim of facilitating broader adoption of RL finetuning of VLA models in robotics.

</details>


### [51] [RepSAM: Bridging Foundation Models to Robotic Vision via Representation-Guided Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25495)
*Wenhui Chu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: RepSAM is a representation-guided PEFT framework that adapts foundation models (SAM) to robotic perception by using a CKA-guided rank allocation strategy and multi-modal fusion. It achieves 97.9% of full fine-tuning performance (89.0% vs 90.9% mIoU) with 158x fewer parameters (4.0M vs 632M), 96x faster training (4 vs 384 GPU-hours), and 12% improvement in robotic manipulation success rates over LoRA baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robotic perception in unstructured environments is challenging. Foundation models like SAM exhibit performance degradation due to non-uniform representation shifts across transformer layers—shallow layers suffer from large domain gaps (CKA < 0.5) while deep layers transfer well (CKA > 0.7). Existing PEFT methods fail to account for this layer-wise heterogeneity.

Method: RepSAM uses a theoretically grounded CKA-guided rank allocation strategy that assigns different ranks to different layers based on their representation shift (measured by CKA similarity), combined with a multi-modal fusion module for handling challenging scenarios like transparent objects and cluttered scenes.

Result: 89.0% vs 90.9% mIoU (97.9% of full FT), 158x parameter reduction (632M→4.0M), 96x training time reduction (384→4 GPU-hours), 7.9% mIoU improvement over DoRA, 12.0% absolute improvement in robotic manipulation success rates over LoRA (RGB) baseline, all statistically significant (p < 0.01).

Conclusion: RepSAM demonstrates that layer-wise adaptive PEFT guided by representation similarity (CKA) is an effective and efficient strategy for adapting foundation models to robotic vision, particularly in unstructured environments with transparent objects and clutter.

Abstract: Robotic perception in unstructured environments remains challenging despite the zero-shot capabilities of foundation models such as SAM. This work attributes performance degradation to non-uniform representation shifts across transformer layers: shallow layers exhibit substantial domain gaps (CKA < 0.5), whereas deep layers transfer effectively (CKA > 0.7). Based on this observation, we propose RepSAM, a representation-guided parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) framework for adapting foundation models to robotic vision. RepSAM employs a theoretically grounded CKA-guided rank allocation strategy combined with a multi-modal fusion module for robust handling of challenging robotic scenarios, including transparent objects and cluttered scenes. Experimental evaluation across six benchmarks and robotic manipulation tasks demonstrates that RepSAM achieves 97.9% of full fine-tuning performance (89.0% vs. 90.9% mIoU) while reducing trainable parameters by 158x (from 632M to 4.0M). RepSAM outperforms DoRA by 7.9% mIoU with just 4 hours of training on a single A100 GPU (a 96x reduction from full fine-tuning, which takes 384 GPU-hours). These improvements are statistically significant (p < 0.01) and translate to a 12.0% absolute improvement in robotic manipulation success rates over the LoRA (RGB) baseline.

</details>


### [52] [Action-Prior Denoising for Smooth Real-Time Chunking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25537)
*Dongyang Liu,Zhaowen Zheng,Yu Sun,Longxu Zhang,Yixuan Liu,Hao Wan*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Soft RTC improves training-time real-time chunking by using action-prior denoising with soft overlap constraints, achieving competitive solve rates while reducing high-delay action delta and jerk.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Training-time RTC uses a binary prefix mask that treats all non-prefix tokens as fully unconstrained, which under-models asynchronous execution where early overlap actions are fixed but later overlap actions should stay close to the previous plan.

Method: Soft RTC constructs corrupted overlap tokens from partially denoised states instead of pure noise and injects the aligned previous chunk as a prior during inference via a lightweight token-wise blending rule.

Result: On 12 Kinetix levels, a short soft window nearly matches hard training-time RTC in solve rate (0.809 vs 0.815); a medium window reduces high-delay action delta by 9.1% and jerk by 9.6%. A real-robot sorting study shows Soft RTC gives the lowest commanded-action finite-difference metrics.

Conclusion: Training-time RTC can improve task completion, and Soft RTC provides smoother action execution with near-naive runtime, unlike inference-time RTC baselines.

Abstract: Real-time chunking (RTC) lets chunked action policies operate under inference delay by conditioning a newly generated action chunk on actions already committed by the previous chunk. Training-time RTC simulates this delay during learning and avoids expensive guidance at deployment, but its binary prefix mask treats all non-prefix tokens as fully unconstrained. This under-models asynchronous execution: early overlap actions are fixed, while later overlap actions remain editable but should still stay close to the previous plan. We propose Soft RTC, a training-time RTC generalization based on action-prior denoising. Soft RTC constructs corrupted overlap tokens from partially denoised states instead of pure noise and injects the aligned previous chunk as the same prior during inference through a lightweight token-wise blending rule. On the 12 released large Kinetix levels, a short soft window nearly matches hard training-time RTC in overall solve rate (0.809 vs. 0.815), while a medium window reduces high-delay action delta and jerk by 9.1% and 9.6% relative to hard RTC. Both variants keep near-naive runtime, unlike inference-time RTC baselines. A small preliminary real-robot sorting study provides additional evidence that training-time RTC can improve completion and that Soft RTC gives the lowest commanded-action finite-difference metrics among the tested policies.

</details>


### [53] [Safety-Critical Whole-Body Control for Humanoid Robots via Input-to-State Safe Control Barrier Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25546)
*Kwanwoo Lee,Sanghyuk Park,Gyeongjae Park,Myeong-Ju Kim,Jaeheung Park*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A hierarchical safety-critical whole-body control framework for humanoid robots using input-to-state safe control barrier functions (ISSf-CBFs) that ensures kinematic safety constraints under unknown disturbances, validated through simulation and real-robot experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing safety-critical control approaches for humanoid robots lack robustness against unknown disturbances (model uncertainties, tracking errors, external perturbations), degrading kinematic safety guarantees during real-world operation.

Method: A three-layer hierarchical framework: (1) Kinematic-level Whole-Body Controller (KinWBC) generates nominal joint references from prioritized tasks; (2) ISSf-CBF safety filter minimally modifies references to satisfy kinematic safety under bounded disturbances; (3) Dynamic-level Whole-Body Controller (DynWBC) tracks filtered references while enforcing dynamic feasibility and contact stability. ISSf-CBF parameters are conservatively tuned to transfer kinematic guarantees to full-order dynamics.

Result: Improved safety margins under model mismatch and reliable real-time enforcement of multiple safety constraints during locomotion, teleoperation, and single-leg balancing with hand control in both simulation and real-robot experiments.

Conclusion: The proposed ISSf-CBF-based hierarchical framework effectively bridges the gap between kinematic safety guarantees and full-order humanoid dynamics, providing robust safety-critical control under unknown disturbances for practical humanoid robot operation.

Abstract: Safety-critical control is essential for humanoid robots operating in complex human-centered environments, where physical safety constraints such as joint limits, self-collision avoidance, obstacle avoidance, and workspace boundaries must be satisfied during real-robot operation. However, existing approaches remain limited because kinematic safety guarantees can be degraded in the presence of unknown disturbances, such as model uncertainties, trajectory-tracking errors, and external perturbations. This paper presents a hierarchical safety-critical whole-body control framework for humanoid robots based on input-to-state safe control barrier functions (ISSf-CBFs). The proposed architecture integrates a kinematic-level whole-body controller (KinWBC), an ISSf-CBF safety filter, and a dynamic-level whole-body controller (DynWBC). KinWBC generates nominal joint-motion references from prioritized tasks; the ISSf-CBF filter minimally modifies these references to satisfy kinematic safety constraints under bounded disturbances; and DynWBC tracks the filtered references while enforcing full-body dynamic feasibility and contact stability. Safety constraints are imposed on a whole-body kinematic model, and the ISSf-CBF parameters are conservatively tuned so that the resulting kinematic safety guarantees can be transferred to full-order humanoid dynamics under unknown disturbances. Simulation and real-robot experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework improves safety margins under model mismatch and reliably enforces multiple safety constraints in real time during locomotion, teleoperation, and single-leg balancing with hand control. Project website: https://kwlee365.github.io/SafeWBC-Website/

</details>


### [54] [TapSampling: Inference-Time Sampling with a Task-Progress-Understanding Verifier for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25547)
*Sizhe Zhao,Shengping Zhang,Shuo Yang,Weiyu Zhao,Shuigen Wang,Xiangyang Ji*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: TapSampling is a plug-and-play inference-time sampling framework that improves embodied control policies by (1) using an Action-VAE to sample diverse candidate actions from a latent space, and (2) selecting the best action via a task-progress verifier, without requiring policy finetuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing embodied control research focuses on scaling training data and model size, but overlooks inference-time strategies as an alternative axis for improvement. The single-shot inference paradigm of non-deterministic generative models (diffusion, autoregressive) limits their performance.

Method: TapSampling consists of two components: (1) an Action-VAE that encodes policy-generated initial actions into a low-dimensional latent space, enabling efficient sampling and decoding of diverse candidate actions; (2) an action verifier trained as a task-progress outcome predictor using the sequential structure of robotic datasets, enabling interpretable action selection. The framework is policy-agnostic and requires no policy finetuning.

Result: Extensive experiments in both simulated and real-world environments show that TapSampling substantially improves multiple generalist policies without additional finetuning.

Conclusion: Inference-time sampling is an effective and orthogonal axis to scaling data/model size for improving embodied control performance. TapSampling provides a general, plug-and-play solution that enhances existing policies.

Abstract: Existing embodied control research demonstrates remarkable performance improvements by scaling training data and model size. We instead explore inference-time strategy as an alternative axis. Non-deterministic generative models, such as diffusion and autoregressive models, have been widely adopted in the field of embodied control. However, the single-shot inference paradigm limits their performance. In this paper, we propose \textbf{TapSampling}, a plug-and-play framework for inference-time sampling. First, we introduce an Action-VAE that represents actions in a low-dimensional latent space by mapping policy-generated initial actions into a compressed posterior distribution, from which any number of latent samples can be drawn and decoded into candidate actions that approximate the true action distribution. Second, we formulate action verification as task-progress outcome prediction, using the intrinsic sequential structure of robotic datasets to train a semantically grounded verifier for interpretable action selection. Furthermore, TapSampling is a policy-agnostic framework. Extensive experiments in both simulated and real-world environments demonstrate that our method substantially improves multiple generalist policies without further policy finetuning. Code and models are available at the project page.

</details>


### [55] [Acting on the Unseen: Communication-Free Collaborative Filtering for Decentralized Multi-Robot Task Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25584)
*Alexander Apartsin,Yigal Meshulam,Yehudit Aperstein*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: This paper introduces Zero-Knowledge MRTA (ZK-MRTA), a multi-robot task allocation regime where robots have no prior knowledge, no communication, and only partial noisy observations of teammates' outcomes. The proposed SwarmCF algorithm uses online low-rank collaborative filtering over a broadcast stream to enable robots to perform well on unseen tasks, achieving Θ(d) per-robot sample complexity (vs. Θ(n) for structure-free learners) and recovering ~80% of centralized full-communication performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multi-robot task allocation assumes communication, known task models, or a coordinator, but many practical scenarios lack these. The paper aims to address the overlooked regime where robots have zero prior knowledge, no communication, and only partial noisy observations.

Method: The paper proposes SwarmCF, an online low-rank collaborative filtering algorithm that exploits a hidden low-rank structure governing robot-task suitability. Each robot runs online low-rank collaborative filtering over the broadcast of teammates' outcomes to infer task suitability for unseen pairs.

Result: Structure-free learners are provably at the prior-mean error floor on unseen pairs. SwarmCF achieves Θ(d) vs Θ(n) per-robot sample complexity, an anytime cumulative-reward separation under task scarcity, and exact decentralized recovery from masked broadcast under a deterministic condition. Empirically recovers ~80% of centralized full-communication ceiling, exhibits positive scaling law with team size, and holds under capacity-1 contention.

Conclusion: The ZK-MRTA regime is tractable despite extreme constraints, and low-rank collaborative filtering via SwarmCF provides categorical advantages over structure-free learning, validated both theoretically and empirically.

Abstract: Multi-robot task allocation usually assumes some combination of communication, known task models, or a coordinator.
